**Interfaces:**

- **Consumes:**
  - The session read's `income_window` (Task 3): `{"start": "YYYY-MM-DD", "end": "YYYY-MM-DD"}`.
  - From Task 2: `income_info.business_and_rental` and `spouse_income.business_and_rental`, where null is unanswered and `[]` is none. The PATCH reply lists each in the order sent, each entry with its `id`. Entry amounts are decimal strings or null. `monthly_rental_income` is read-only.
  - `SOURCE_FIELDS` order (Task 2).
- **Produces (types, `types/api.ts`):**
  - `INCOME_FIELDS`, in Task 2's `SOURCE_FIELDS` order: `monthly_gross_wages`, `monthly_overtime`, `monthly_tips`, `monthly_interest_and_dividends`, `monthly_pension`, `monthly_social_security`, `monthly_unemployment`, `monthly_child_support`, `monthly_alimony`, `monthly_other_income`.
  - `type IncomeField = (typeof INCOME_FIELDS)[number]`.
  - `type EntryKind = 'business' | 'rental'`.
  - `interface IncomeEntry { id?: number; kind: EntryKind | null; description: string | null; gross: number | null; expenses: number | null; current_gross: number | null; current_expenses: number | null }`.
  - `ENTRY_AMOUNT_KEYS = ['gross', 'expenses', 'current_gross', 'current_expenses'] as const`.
  - `interface IncomePeriod { start: string; end: string }`.
  - `IntakeSession.income_window: IncomePeriod`.
  - `IncomeInfo` changes:
    - it gains `monthly_interest_and_dividends?: number | null`, `business_and_rental?: IncomeEntry[] | null` and `readonly income_period_end?: string | null`;
    - `monthly_rental_income` becomes `readonly monthly_rental_income?: number | null`, with a comment that it is read-only and unread by the step.
  - `export type SpouseIncome = Partial<Omit<IncomeInfo, 'id' | 'session' | 'data_source' | 'monthly_income' | 'marital_status' | 'income_period_end' | 'total_monthly_income'>>`. Every key Tasks 6–8 add to `IncomeInfo` reaches the spouse under the same name (§4), and a `SpouseIncome` stays assignable to `Partial<IncomeInfo>`.
- **Produces (utils):**
  - `formatIsoDate(iso: string): string` in `utils/formatIsoDate.ts`.
  - In `utils/incomeEntries.ts`:
    - `type EntryRow = WizardRow<IncomeEntry>` (exported).
    - `MAX_ENTRIES = 10`.
    - `newIncomeEntry(key: string): EntryRow` returns `{ _key: key, kind: null, description: null, gross: null, expenses: null, current_gross: null, current_expenses: null }`.
    - `isEntryTouched(row: EntryRow): boolean` is true when `row.id !== undefined` or any of the six answers is non-null.
    - `sentEntry(row: EntryRow): EntryRow` rebuilds the row in this order: `_key`; `id` if any; then `kind`, `description`, `gross`, `expenses`, `current_gross`, `current_expenses`, each `?? null`. `saveCurrentStepData` strips the `_key`.
    - `entriesToSend(rows: EntryRow[], noEntries: boolean): EntryRow[] | null` returns the touched rows through `sentEntry`, in order. With none, it returns `[]` when `noEntries` is true and `null` otherwise.
    - `hydrateEntries(entries: IncomeEntry[] | null | undefined): EntryRow[]` maps each entry to `{ _key: String(e.id), ...hydrateAmounts(e, ENTRY_AMOUNT_KEYS) }`. `null` and `undefined` give `[]`.
    - `kindNet(rows: EntryRow[], kind: EntryKind, current: boolean): number` is the sum, over rows of that kind, of `(gross ?? 0) - (expenses ?? 0)`. With `current`, it reads `current_gross`/`current_expenses` instead.
    - `flooredNet(rows, kind, current): number` is `Math.max(0, kindNet(...))`.
- **Produces (wizard, `IntakeWizard.tsx`):**
  - New state: `entryRows`, `spouseEntryRows: EntryRow[]` and `noEntries`, `spouseNoEntries: boolean`.
  - `listSavesRef = useRef({ assets: 0, debts: 0, members: 0, entries: 0, spouseEntries: 0 })`.
  - `IncomeInfoStep` gains these props: `incomeWindow: IncomePeriod`, `entryRows`, `onEntryRowsChange`, `spouseEntryRows`, `onSpouseEntryRowsChange`, `noEntries`, `onNoEntriesChange`, `spouseNoEntries`, `onSpouseNoEntriesChange`. The wizard passes `session.income_window`.
- **Produces (`IncomeEntryList` props):** `rows`, `onRowsChange(rows)`, `noEntries`, `onNoEntriesChange(pressed)`, `spouse: boolean`, and `errors: Record<string, string>` keyed by box name.
- **Produces (words), `copy.ts`.** Each string here scores 9 or under by `reading-level.test.ts`'s formula, checked by hand.
  - **The window and the list:**
    - `INCOME_WINDOW_LEAD = 'Your average monthly income for'` (`// DRAFT: counsel C-10`), 7.60.
    - `SPOUSE_INCOME_WINDOW_LEAD = "Your spouse's average monthly income for"` (C-10, plan draft), 5.68.
    - `INCOME_AVERAGE_HEADING = 'Your income: the six-month average'` (plan draft), 4.45.
    - `ENTRIES_HEADING = 'Businesses and rental property'` (plan draft, not scored).
    - `ADD_ENTRY = '+ Add a business or property'`, 7.60.
    - `SPOUSE_ADD_ENTRY = "+ Add your spouse's business or property"` (plan draft), 5.68.
    - `NO_ENTRIES = 'I have no business or rental income.'`, 5.68.
    - `SPOUSE_NO_ENTRIES = 'My spouse has no business or rental income.'` (plan draft), 5.23.
    - `ENTRY_DESCRIPTION_HELP = "What it is, such as rideshare driving or the property's address"` (plan draft), 6.79.
    - `ENTRY_NET_LABEL = 'Net a month'` (plan draft, not scored).
  - **The averages floor sentences**, each marked `// DRAFT: counsel C-12`:
    - `FLOOR_AVERAGE_BUSINESS = 'For your business income, your operating expenses are more than your gross receipts. DigniFi counts this net as $0.00 on Form 122A-1, and on Schedule I unless your income is now different.'` (§4.2), 6.71.
    - `FLOOR_AVERAGE_RENTAL`: the same sentence with "rental" for "business".
    - `SPOUSE_FLOOR_AVERAGE_BUSINESS = "For your spouse's business income, the operating expenses are more than the gross receipts. DigniFi counts this net as $0.00 on Form 122A-1, and on Schedule I unless your spouse's income is now different."` (plan draft), 6.49.
    - `SPOUSE_FLOOR_AVERAGE_RENTAL`: the same with "rental".
  - **The double-count note:** `DOUBLE_COUNT_INCOME_NOTE = 'If one cost is both for this work and for your home, count each dollar once, in one place only: here or on the Expenses step. How you split it is your choice. Legal aid can help.'` (`// DRAFT: counsel C-13`, §4.2), 2.61.
- **Produces (words), `formText.ts`.** No test scores it. Each constant gets a comment naming its source (`b_122a-1.pdf` page 1), as the file's convention asks.
  - `AVERAGING_SENTENCE = 'If the amount of your monthly income varied during the 6 months, add the income for all 6 months and divide the total by 6.'`
  - `ONE_COLUMN_ONLY = 'Do not include any income amount more than once. For example, if both spouses own the same rental property, put the income from that property in one column only.'` These are the template's two sentences whole.
  - `ENTRY_KIND_WORDS: Record<EntryKind, string> = { business: 'A business, profession, or farm, or self-employment', rental: 'Rental or other real property' }` (§4.2's "Asked as").
  - `INTEREST_LABEL = 'Interest, dividends, and royalties'` (122A-1 line 7).
  - `GROSS_RECEIPTS_LABEL = 'Gross receipts (before all deductions)'` (122A-1 lines 5 and 6).
  - `OPERATING_EXPENSES_LABEL = 'Ordinary and necessary operating expenses'` (lines 5 and 6).
- **Produces (locators: E2E and the tests).** The prefix `spouse_` stands before each spouse name.
  - Entry `i`: select `entry_kind_<i>`, text `entry_description_<i>` (`maxLength` 200), amounts `entry_gross_<i>` and `entry_expenses_<i>`.
  - Each card gets a Remove button with `aria-label` `Remove business or property <i+1>`. The spouse's reads `Remove your spouse's business or property <i+1>`.
  - The interest box is `monthly_interest_and_dividends` (`spouse_monthly_interest_and_dividends`).
- **Produces (E2E):**
  - `IncomeSourceKey` replaces `'monthly_rental_income'` with `'monthly_interest_and_dividends'`.
  - `interface PersonaEntry { kind: 'business' | 'rental'; description: string; gross: number; expenses: number }`.
  - `PersonaIncome` gains `business: PersonaEntry[]` and `spouse_business?: PersonaEntry[]`.
- **CLAUDE.md:** "Autosave sends only real edits" (the step 2 body) and "Nested debts and assets save by id" (the entries' `_key` and pairing). The gotcha edits are given below, as the sentences read after this task.

`formatIsoDate.test.ts`, "dates read whole, with the year, from the ISO parts" (§4.4):

```ts
expect(formatIsoDate('2026-04-01')).toBe('April 1, 2026');
expect(formatIsoDate('2027-02-28')).toBe('February 28, 2027');
expect(formatIsoDate('2028-02-29')).toBe('February 29, 2028');
expect(formatIsoDate('2026-09-30')).toBe('September 30, 2026');
```

`incomeEntries.test.ts`:

1. **"an entry nobody answered is not sent, and an unanswered list stays null".** `entriesToSend([newIncomeEntry('new-1')], false)` is `null`. With `true` it is `[]`.
2. **"a touched entry is sent with every question, null for the unanswered ones".** `entriesToSend([{ ...newIncomeEntry('new-1'), kind: 'business' }], false)` `toEqual([{ _key: 'new-1', kind: 'business', description: null, gross: null, expenses: null, current_gross: null, current_expenses: null }])`.
3. **"a loaded entry is always sent, with its id".** `entriesToSend(hydrateEntries([{ id: 4, kind: null, description: null, gross: null, expenses: null, current_gross: null, current_expenses: null }]), false)` has one row with `id: 4` and `_key: '4'`.
4. **"loaded amounts become numbers, so they add"** (Review Focus 1). `hydrateEntries([{ id: 1, kind: 'business', description: 'Rideshare and delivery driving', gross: '4200.00', expenses: '830.00', current_gross: null, current_expenses: null }] as unknown as IncomeEntry[])`:
   - `[0].gross` is `4200` and `[0].expenses` is `830`;
   - `kindNet(rows, 'business', false)` is `3370`.
5. **"the floor is on each kind's sum, never one entry, and a loss never crosses kinds"** (§4.2). Use rows business gross 1000 / expenses 1500, business 2000 / 1200, and rental 500 / 900.
   - `kindNet(…, 'business', false)` is `300`, and `flooredNet` gives `300`.
   - `kindNet(…, 'rental', false)` is `-400`, and `flooredNet` gives `0`.
   - The same rows with only the current pair set: `kindNet(…, 'business', true)` reads the current pair, and blank counts as 0.
6. **"a body built from loaded rows equals one built again"** (§7.4). `JSON.stringify(entriesToSend(hydrateEntries(x), false))` is equal twice over for the fixture in test 4, and its first row's keys, in order, are `['_key', 'id', 'kind', 'description', 'gross', 'expenses', 'current_gross', 'current_expenses']`.

**`IncomeEntryList`** follows `HouseholdList` (§4.2, §7.1 section 4):

- **The heading.** `ENTRIES_HEADING`. Under the spouse's list, `ONE_COLUMN_ONLY` as a quotation.
- **Cards.** One card per row, keyed by `row._key`, with title "Business or property <i+1>" and a Remove button. Each card holds:
  - `entry_kind_<i>`: a `FormSelect` with options from `ENTRY_KIND_WORDS`. Blank is `null`.
  - `entry_description_<i>`: labelled 'What it is', with `ENTRY_DESCRIPTION_HELP`. It stores `null` when emptied, never `''`.
  - `entry_gross_<i>`: labelled `GROSS_RECEIPTS_LABEL`, with help `AVERAGING_SENTENCE` for the filer only.
  - `entry_expenses_<i>`: labelled `OPERATING_EXPENSES_LABEL`, with help `DOUBLE_COUNT_INCOME_NOTE` for the filer only (W-4).
  - `NOT_ANSWERED_YET` beside each null answer on a touched row.
  - 'Net a month' and the entry's own `gross − expenses`, in `formatTotal`'s form with a leading `-` below $0.
- **The `spouse_` prefix** goes on every name and label when `spouse` is set.
- **Below the cards:**
  - the averages floor sentence for each kind whose `kindNet(rows, kind, false) < 0`, business before rental, as an `info-box`;
  - `ADD_ENTRY` or `SPOUSE_ADD_ENTRY`, disabled at `MAX_ENTRIES`;
  - the `NO_ENTRIES` or `SPOUSE_NO_ENTRIES` toggle, with `aria-pressed`, shown only while no row is touched.
- **Keys.** A new row's key is `new-${++nextKey}` from a module-level counter in `IncomeEntryList.tsx`, as `AssetsStep.tsx:36` does. The counter is never per mount: the step remounts on each visit while unsaved rows stay in the wizard.
- **Every answer** calls `onRowsChange` and `onNoEntriesChange(false)`. Nothing calls them from an effect.

**`IncomeInfoStep`:**

- **The amount sections**, in display order (§7.1 section 3). They replace `:62-132`. Titles and descriptions stay inline literals, as today, so `reading-level.test.ts` doesn't score them:
  1. `'Employment Income'`, unchanged: wages; then overtime and tips.
  2. `'Support and Interest'`, with description `'Include child support and alimony you receive, and interest and dividends.'`: child support and alimony, with their help unchanged; then `monthly_interest_and_dividends`, labelled `INTEREST_LABEL`.
  3. `'Government Benefits'`, with description `'Include Social Security and unemployment.'`: Social Security and unemployment.
  4. `'Other Income'`, with description `'Include pensions and any other regular income.'`: pension; then other.
- **Above them**, a `form-section` headed `INCOME_AVERAGE_HEADING` with `` `${INCOME_WINDOW_LEAD} ${formatIsoDate(incomeWindow.start)} through ${formatIsoDate(incomeWindow.end)}` `` and `AVERAGING_SENTENCE`. The explainer at `:337-342` stays.

- **`saveCurrentStepData`** replaces the members-only branch at `:462-509`. Every list in the body is paired, and the function returns once, at the end (advisor finding):
  1. **Before the PATCH.** For each list the body carries, strip `_key` from an array with `withoutKey` and take `++listSavesRef.current.<list>`. A null list counts too, as `sendsMembers` does. The lists are `household.members` (`members`), `income_info.business_and_rental` (`entries`) and `spouse_income.business_and_rental` (`spouseEntries`).
  2. **After the reply,** take each array list whose counter hasn't moved on. Pair it with `savedIds(rows, <the reply's list>)`, from `saved.household?.members`, `saved.income_info?.business_and_rental` or `saved.spouse_income?.business_and_rental`, cast as `:502` does. Then call its setter with `withSavedIds`.
  3. **The returned copy** is `body` with each paired list replaced by `withSavedIds(rows, ids).map(sentMember | sentEntry)`. A stale list keeps its sent rows. If no list was paired and any was stale, return nothing, as today.
  4. **The assets and debts branches** stay as they are, since they never share a body with these lists.

```text
feat(intake): ask the six-month averages and list businesses and rentals

The Income step names the six months the averages cover, asks interest
in Form 122A-1's words after support and before unemployment, and lists
each business and rental property with its gross receipts and operating
expenses. Each entry shows its own net; a kind whose summed net is below
$0 says DigniFi counts it as $0.00. A new entry keeps the id its first
save gave it, and showing the step again sends nothing.

Spec: §4.4 (the question, the current figures, "Start from my average figures", no pre-fill), §4.6 (the questions), §4.2 (the current-figures floor sentences), §7.1 sections 5–7 (their spouse twins), §7.4 (the current ten only while "yes", and the five assistance fields), §7.5. Lines are as Task 6 left them. Find each by the code quoted.

**Files:**

- **Modify:**
  - `frontend/src/types/api.ts`: `IncomeInfo`, and new constants after `INCOME_FIELDS`.
  - `frontend/src/utils/incomeEntries.ts`: `startFromAverages`.
  - `frontend/src/components/wizard/steps/IncomeInfoStep.tsx`: sections 5 and 6 and their spouse twins, errors, and props unchanged.

  - `frontend/src/components/wizard/steps/__tests__/IncomeInfoStep.test.tsx`
  - `frontend/src/pages/__tests__/IntakeWizard.test.tsx`: the expected bodies at the two income-save tests (`:1635-1638` and `:1650-1653` at 5c6fe90), and new tests.

**Interfaces:**

- **Consumes:**
  - Task 6's `EntryRow`, `IncomeEntryList`, `kindNet` and the step-2 body.
  - Task 2's keys: `income_now_different` and `assistance_answer` (`"yes"`/`"no"`/null; anything else is a 400); the current ten `current_<source>`; `cash_assistance_kind`, `noncash_assistance_kind` (text, at most 100, `""` when blank); `cash_assistance_amount` and `noncash_assistance_value` (amounts or null).
- **Produces (types):**
  - `type YesNo = 'yes' | 'no'`.
  - `CURRENT_INCOME_FIELDS = ['current_monthly_gross_wages', …, 'current_monthly_other_income'] as const`, spelled out in `INCOME_FIELDS` order.
  - `ASSISTANCE_AMOUNT_FIELDS = ['cash_assistance_amount', 'noncash_assistance_value'] as const` (plan-added, W-5).
  - `IncomeInfo` gains `income_now_different?: YesNo | null`, the ten `current_*?: number | null`, `assistance_answer?: YesNo | null`, `cash_assistance_kind?: string`, `cash_assistance_amount?: number | null`, `noncash_assistance_kind?: string` and `noncash_assistance_value?: number | null`. `SpouseIncome` follows through Task 6's `Omit`.
- **Produces (`incomeEntries.ts`):**
  - `startFromAverages<D extends Partial<IncomeInfo>>(data: D, rows: EntryRow[]): { data: D; rows: EntryRow[] }`. Each `current_<f>` becomes `data[f] ?? null`, so a blank stays blank and is never 0. Each row's `current_gross` becomes `gross ?? null` and its `current_expenses` becomes `expenses ?? null`. Nothing else changes.
- **Produces (step 2 body, §7.4 exactly, in this key order):**
  - `income_info`:
    1. the ten;
    2. `marital_status`;
    3. `business_and_rental`;
    4. `income_now_different ?? null`;
    5. only while `income_now_different === 'yes'`, the ten `current_<f> ?? null`;
    6. `assistance_answer ?? null`, `cash_assistance_kind ?? ''`, `cash_assistance_amount ?? null`, `noncash_assistance_kind ?? ''`, `noncash_assistance_value ?? null`.
  - `spouse_income`: the same without `marital_status`, gated on the spouse's own `income_now_different`.
- **Produces (words), `copy.ts`.** Each scores 9 or under.

    - `NOW_DIFFERENT_QUESTION = 'Is your income now different from that average?'` (6.71);

Include cash assistance and the value (if known) of any non-cash assistance that you receive, such as food stamps (benefits under the Supplemental Nutrition Assistance Program) or housing subsidies.'` The cut is "Specify:", from `form_b106i.pdf` 8f.
- **Produces (locators).** `spouse_` goes before each name for the spouse.

start empty, with a button that copies the averages. Each person also
answers Schedule I 8f's question, with cash and non-cash help kept
apart. The current figures are sent only while the answer is Yes, so
answering No keeps them stored and unread.
```

**Backend, `test_data_migrations.py`** (beside the other intake data migrations, which share its `migrate` fixture and `_session`; importing the fixture into a new file would trip ruff F811, W-12). New constants after `:23`: `BEFORE = [("intake", "0027_expenseinfo_schedule_j_lines")]` and `SHIFT = [("intake", "0028_shift_current_step")]`.

```python
def _run_the_shift(migrate):
    apps = migrate(BEFORE)
    IntakeSession = apps.get_model("intake", "IntakeSession")
    first = _session(apps)
    rows = {step: IntakeSession.objects.create(user_id=first.user_id, district_id=first.district_id,
                                               current_step=step)
            for step in (1, 2, 3, 8, 0, 12)}
    apps = migrate(SHIFT)
    after = {step: apps.get_model("intake", "IntakeSession").objects.get(pk=row.pk).current_step
             for step, row in rows.items()}
    take_home = apps.get_model("intake", "IntakeSession").objects.create(
        user_id=first.user_id, district_id=first.district_id, current_step=3)
    apps = migrate(BEFORE)
    undone = {step: apps.get_model("intake", "IntakeSession").objects.get(pk=row.pk).current_step
              for step, row in rows.items()}
    return after, undone, apps.get_model("intake", "IntakeSession").objects.get(pk=take_home.pk).current_step

def test_a_session_saved_before_take_home_pay_reopens_on_the_same_step(migrate):
    after, undone, take_home = _run_the_shift(migrate)
    assert after == {1: 1, 2: 2, 3: 4, 8: 9, 0: 0, 12: 12}
    assert undone == {1: 1, 2: 2, 3: 3, 8: 8, 0: 0, 12: 12}
    assert take_home == 3  # Take-home pay undone reads as the old Expenses
```

`_session(apps)` creates a first session at step 1 (`current_step` default). The dict comprehension's step 1 row is a second session. Its twin, `..._on_postgres`, is marked `postgres` with the `skipif` of `test_data_migrations.py:256-260`, and asserts the same.

  }

    LINE_4_GROUP_HEADING,
    LINE_15_GROUP_HEADING,
    LINE_20_GROUP_HEADING,
    NONCASH_EXPENSES_SENTENCE,
    DOUBLE_COUNT_EXPENSES_NOTE,
    LINE_3_INFO,
  ]) {
    expect(screen.getByText(words)).toBeInTheDocument();
  }
});

  expect([no.checked, yes.checked]).toEqual([false, false]);
  expect(lastEmitted().expenses_include_others ?? null).toBeNull();
  expect(
    line3.compareDocumentPosition(inputNamed('rent_or_mortgage')!) &

  ).toBeTruthy();

  expect(lastEmitted().expenses_include_others).toBe('yes');
});

});

  ['other_expenses', 'other_expenses_specify'],
] as const)(

      (i) => i.name
    );

    expect(inputNamed(specify)).toHaveAttribute('maxlength', '100');

    });

  }
);

Do not include insurance deducted from your pay or included in lines 4 or 20.'`; `LINE_20_GROUP_HEADING = 'Other real property expenses not included in lines 4 or 5 of this form or on Schedule I: Your Income.'`; `NONCASH_EXPENSES_SENTENCE = 'Include expenses paid for with non-cash government assistance if you know the value of such assistance and have included it on Schedule I.'`. - [ ] **Step 5: Rebuild `ExpenseInfoStep`**

The file keeps its props (`initialData`, `onDataChange`, `onValidationChange`), its amount validation through `amountError(…, 10)` for each of `EXPENSE_FIELDS`, and its info box. It renders in this order:

The other five apply §8's rule literally, to every "your" and "you receive" in the filer's sentence: "the spouse's sentences swap "your" for "your spouse's" and "you receive" for "your spouse receives", and the plan writes all seven out" (spec §8, "What the gaps test"). So `SPOUSE_CURRENT_NOT_GIVEN` reads "change your spouse's answer", though the filer gives that answer. Two can't come from the rule, since the filer's sentence has no "your" or "you receive" to swap: `SPOUSE_ENTRIES_UNANSWERED` and `SPOUSE_ENTRY_UNANSWERED`.

  ```python
  ENTRIES_UNANSWERED = "Tell us about any business or rental property on the Income step, or say you have none."
  ENTRY_UNANSWERED = "Answer every question for each business or rental property on the Income step."
  NOW_DIFFERENT_UNANSWERED = "Answer whether your income is now different from the six-month average, on the Income step."
  CURRENT_NOT_GIVEN = "Enter your income now on the Income step, or change your answer about whether it is different."
  ASSISTANCE_UNANSWERED = "Answer whether you receive other government assistance, on the Income step."
  ASSISTANCE_KIND_MISSING = "Say what government assistance you receive on the Income step."
  CASH_AMOUNT_MISSING = "Enter how much cash assistance you receive a month, on the Income step."
  # Plan drafts for the owner: the spouse's sentences (3e2 spec §8)
  SPOUSE_ENTRIES_UNANSWERED = "Tell us about any business or rental property your spouse has on the Income step, or say your spouse has none."
  SPOUSE_ENTRY_UNANSWERED = "Answer every question for each of your spouse's businesses or rental properties on the Income step."
  SPOUSE_NOW_DIFFERENT_UNANSWERED = "Answer whether your spouse's income is now different from the six-month average, on the Income step."
  SPOUSE_CURRENT_NOT_GIVEN = "Enter your spouse's income now on the Income step, or change your spouse's answer about whether it is different."
  SPOUSE_ASSISTANCE_UNANSWERED = "Answer whether your spouse receives other government assistance, on the Income step."
  SPOUSE_ASSISTANCE_KIND_MISSING = "Say what government assistance your spouse receives on the Income step."
  SPOUSE_CASH_AMOUNT_MISSING = "Enter how much cash assistance your spouse receives a month, on the Income step."
  ```

  Also `_detail_gaps(row, *, spouse: bool) -> list[IncomeStepGap]`, which returns one person's gaps in that order and treats `row is None` as a row with every answer null.

- [ ] **Step 6: Implement the gaps in `cmi.py`**

Add the 14 constants exactly as Interfaces spells them, with its `# Plan drafts for the owner` comment above the seven `SPOUSE_` ones, and `_detail_gaps(row, *, spouse)`. Per person, in order:

1. The list is None → ENTRIES_UNANSWERED.
2. Otherwise, any entry with `not is_entry_answered(entry, current=uses_current(row))` → ENTRY_UNANSWERED.
3. `income_now_different` None → NOW_DIFFERENT_UNANSWERED.
4. `"yes"` and `not current_given(row)` → CURRENT_NOT_GIVEN.
5. `assistance_answer` None → ASSISTANCE_UNANSWERED.
6. `"yes"` and (both kinds blank once stripped, or `cash_assistance_amount` not None beside a blank cash kind, or `noncash_assistance_value` not None beside a blank non-cash kind) → ASSISTANCE_KIND_MISSING.
7. `"yes"`, a cash kind not blank and `cash_assistance_amount` None → CASH_AMOUNT_MISSING.

Steps 6 and 7 can both fire for one person. Call `_detail_gaps` from `income_step_gaps` in the Interfaces' order. The function still never raises.

- Produces (backend):
  - `_income_period(session) -> dict` in `apps/forms/views.py`, returning `{"answered_for": {"start", "end"} | None, "now": {"start", "end"}}`. Dates are ISO strings. `answered_for` is None with no income row or a null stamp.

The wizard passes `session?.income_info?.answered_window ?? null` from the context's session. `updateCurrentStep` replaces that session with `update_step`'s reply (`IntakeContext.tsx:150-155`), so the stamp Continue sets reaches the step when the filer goes back. `incomeData`, hydrated once at mount, would keep the old window.

def test_take_home_pay_is_gross_less_the_deductions_on_schedule_i_106sum_and_schedule_j(session):
    # wages 3200.00, payroll_tax 400.00, every other deduction blank (§4.5, §9.1, §11 Maria)
    assert {b: schedule_i[b] for b in DEDUCTION_BOXES} == {
        "Amount 5a Debtor 1": "400.00", **dict.fromkeys(DEDUCTION_BOXES[1:], "0.00")}
    assert (schedule_i["Amount 6 Debtor 1"], schedule_i["Amount 7 Debtor 1"],
            schedule_i["Amount 10 Debtor 1"], schedule_i["Amount 12"],
            form_106sum["4"], schedule_j["23a"]) == ("400.00", "2800.00", "2800.00",
            "2800.00", "2800.00", "2800.00")

def test_a_blank_deduction_prints_0_00_and_counts_as_zero(session):
    # payroll_tax 400.00, payroll_union_dues left None
    assert (fm["Amount 5g Debtor 1"], fm["Amount 6 Debtor 1"]) == ("0.00", "400.00")

def test_deductions_larger_than_pay_print_take_home_pay_below_zero(session):
    # wages 100.00, payroll_tax 150.00 (§4.5: "below $0 if the deductions are larger")
    assert fm["Amount 7 Debtor 1"] == "-50.00"

def test_other_deductions_are_named_only_while_5h_is_above_zero(session):
    # payroll_other 25.00, payroll_other_specify "Parking" -> "Other deductions 5h" == "Parking";
    # payroll_other 0.00 with the same text -> "Other deductions 5h" not in the map

def test_schedule_i_prints_income_that_is_now_different_while_122a1_keeps_the_average(session):

    # current_monthly_gross_wages 3600.00, every other current box blank

            schedule_i["Amount 8b Debtor 1"]) == ("3600.00", "0.00", "0.00")
    assert (form_122a1["Debto1.Quest2.0"], form_122a1["Debto1.Quest11"]) == ("3000.00", "3000.00")

def test_schedule_i_reads_the_averages_when_the_answer_moved_back_to_no(session):

    assert schedule_i["Amount 2 Debtor 1"] == "3000.00"

def test_8f_prints_cash_and_noncash_assistance_and_never_changes_cmi(session):

def test_a_kind_typed_under_yes_then_answered_no_prints_0_00_and_a_blank_specify(session):

    # business entry: gross 1000.00, expenses 400.00 (averages), current_gross 300.00,
    # current_expenses 900.00; income_now_different "yes"; wages 0 (§13)

def test_a_business_loss_never_lowers_rental_income_on_8a(session):
    # business 600.00/1100.00 and rental 900.00/200.00 on the averages -> 8a "700.00"

def test_the_8h_words_follow_the_figures_schedule_i_uses(session):
    # averages: monthly_other_income 0.00; "now different" "yes"; current_monthly_other_income 300.00

    # the reverse, on the same row: monthly_other_income 300.00, current_monthly_other_income 0.00,

    assert fm["Amount 8h Debtor 1"] == "0.00" and "Other income 8h" not in fm
    assert DERIVATIONS["other_income_label"](session) == "Other income"  # 122A-1 line 10 keeps the averages

  - `schedule_i_other_income_total_debtor1` adds interest, child support, alimony, unemployment, Social Security, pension and other.

```python
@pytest.mark.parametrize("rule", [f"schedule_i_{n}_{c}" for n in (
    "payroll_tax", "payroll_mandatory_retirement", "payroll_voluntary_retirement",
    "payroll_retirement_loan", "payroll_insurance", "payroll_support", "payroll_union_dues",
    "payroll_other", "total_deductions") for c in ("debtor1", "spouse")] + ["schedule_i_payroll_other_specify"])
def test_a_deduction_refusal_links_to_the_take_home_pay_step(rule):
    assert RULE_FIXES[rule] == {"step": "take_home_pay"}

@pytest.mark.parametrize("rule", ["schedule_i_take_home_pay_debtor1", "schedule_i_take_home_pay_spouse",
    "schedule_i_income_total", "schedule_j_total_income", "schedule_j_monthly_net_income"])
def test_a_rule_that_reads_income_and_take_home_pay_links_to_the_income_step(rule):
    assert RULE_FIXES[rule] == {"step": "income_info"}  # the earlier of its two steps (§9)

def test_the_rental_rules_are_gone():
    assert {"schedule_i_rental_debtor1", "schedule_i_rental_spouse"} & (DERIVATIONS.keys() | RULE_FIXES.keys()) == set()
```

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: `kind_net`, `schedule_i_field`/`uses_current` and `get_entries()` (Tasks 2–3); `counted_spouse_income`; Task 12's 8a rule.
- Produces:

  - `apps/intake/income.py` `statement_rows(filer, spouse) -> list[dict[str, str]]`:
    - keys `owner`, `description`, `gross`, `expenses`, `net`;
    - the filer's entries, then the spouse's when `spouse` is not None, in stored order;
    - `owner` is `"Debtor 1"` or `"Non-filing spouse"`;
    - `description` is `"Business: " + d` or `"Rental property: " + d`;
    - the amounts are the figures Schedule I uses, quantized strings (`"4200.00"`), a null amount printing `"0.00"`;
    - `net` is the entry's own subtraction and may be negative.
    - After a person's entries comes one floor row per kind whose summed net is below 0, business before rental. Its `description` is `"Business total below $0, brought up to $0.00"` or `"Rental property total below $0, brought up to $0.00"`, marked `# DRAFT: counsel C-12`. `gross` and `expenses` are `""`, and `net` is the summed net with its sign turned.
  - `fill_resolver.resolve_binding("income.statement[].<attr>", session) -> list[str]`. `attr` is one of the five keys; any other raises `ValueError`.
  - `fill_resolver.binding_fix(binding: str) -> dict[str, str]`:
    - `answer:<form>.` → `{"page": "/ask/<form>"}`;
    - `sofa.` → `{"page": "/sofa"}`;
    - `household.` and `income.` → `{"step": "income_info"}`;
    - anything else → `{}`.
      `fix_for` calls it for a non-derived, non-ingested field.
  - `schema.is_statement_group(schema: FormSchema, name: str) -> bool`: the group has a `sheet`, no `extra_page`, and no field names it as `repeat`. This name is new to this plan; see Writer notes item 6.
  - `resolve_parts` prints a statement group's rows (capacity 0) as one sheet part, when at least one row exists, after any repeat group's sheet.
  - `sheets._fixes` uses `binding_fix(column.binding)` for a statement group's columns.
  - `_LIST_BINDING = re.compile(r"(?:sofa\.\w+|household\.dependents|income\.statement)\[\]\.\w+")`. `income.` stays out of `_BINDING_NAMESPACES` (`schema.py:199`).
  - The new E007 check: every column of a statement group binds `income.statement[].<attr>`. The line column is measured at rows 1 and 9999.
  - `schedule_i.json` `groups.i_line_8a` (§9.2):

    - `"number": "{n}"`;
    - sheet title `"Official Form 106I, Schedule I: Your Income, line 8a"`;
    - columns (label / binding / width):

      | Label                                      | Binding                          | Width |
      | ------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------- | ----- |
      | `Income of`                                | `income.statement[].owner`       | 96    |
      | `Property or business`                     | `income.statement[].description` | 132   |
      | `Gross receipts`                           | `income.statement[].gross`       | 80    |
      | `Ordinary and necessary business expenses` | `income.statement[].expenses`    | 80    |
      | `Total monthly net income`                 | `income.statement[].net`         | 80    |

  - Andrés, in both the seed and `personas.ts`: `monthly_other_income` 0, and one business entry `{kind: "business", description: "Rideshare and delivery driving", gross: 4200, expenses: 830}`.

- [ ] **Step 4: Implement the engine, in this order, before declaring the group**

1. `income.statement_rows(filer, spouse)`. Amounts read `entry["current_gross"]`/`["current_expenses"]` when `uses_current(row)`, else `gross`/`expenses`. The floor row comes from `kind_net(entries, kind, current=uses_current(row)) < 0`.
2. `resolve_binding`'s `income.` branch. It reads `statement_rows(getattr(session, "income_info", None), counted_spouse_income(session))` and returns the `attr` column. A missing filer row gives `[]`.
3. `binding_fix`, with `fix_for` (`:140-151`) delegating its `answer:`/`sofa.`/`household.` branches to it.
4. `is_statement_group` in `schema.py`.
5. `resolve_parts`: after the repeat groups' loop, for each `name, group` in `schema.groups` with `is_statement_group`:

   - if the lists are non-empty, append a sheet part shaped as `_sheet`'s (`:313-321`), with rows `{"line": group.number.format(n=i), "cells": [col[i - 1] for col in columns]}` from `i = 1`.
6. `sheets._fixes`: when `is_statement_group(schema, group)`, `binding_fix(bindings.get(column.label, ""))`.
7. `schema.py`:
   - extend `_LIST_BINDING`;
   - in `_sheet_errors`, or beside it for statement groups, add the "binds an income.statement[].<attr> list" check;

> A statement group (a `sheet`, no `extra_page`, no field naming it as `repeat`; `schema.is_statement_group`) prints rows from one list binding and has no slots: Schedule I's `i_line_8a`, whose columns bind `income.statement[].<attr>` (`owner`, `description`, `gross`, `expenses`, `net`). "`field_values`, not `form_data`" gains:

feat(forms): Schedule I's line 8a statement on an attached sheet

Every business and rental property each counted person lists prints on
a statement attached to Schedule I: who, what, gross receipts,

sheet group whose columns bind income.statement[]; a refused cell

a business entry, so it prints on 122A-1 line 5 and Schedule I 8a.

- the texts `Other 16`, `Other 19` and `Other 21`, from `taxes_specify` `"2026 estimated income tax"`, `support_others_specify` `"My mother"` and `other_expenses_specify` `"School supplies and fees"`.

Pass the texts through `ExpenseInfo.objects.create`. `VACATED` becomes `{"15a", "15b", "17b", "17c", "22b", "24"}` (§9.4). `test_expense_line_prints_only_above_zero`'s `labels` gains the three texts, so it stores them in its fixture too. The one literal of the old 13-line total, `assert printed == ["8191.00"] * 5` (`test_schedule_j_schema.py:335`, the five boxes that read the total), becomes `assert printed == [EXPENSE_TOTAL] * 5`. Task 12 Step 4 already wrote 23c in `test_net_income_stays_blank_until_income_is_given` as `f"-{EXPENSE_TOTAL}"`, so it moves with the constant.

```python
@pytest.mark.parametrize("answer,ticks", [("yes", "/yes"), ("no", "/no"), (None, None)])
def test_line_3_prints_the_filers_answer_and_never_answers_for_them(session, answer, ticks):
    ExpenseInfo.objects.create(session=session, expenses_include_others=answer)
    assert resolve(load_schema("schedule_j"), session).get("check3") == ticks

@pytest.mark.parametrize("amount_field,text_field,box", [("taxes_not_deducted", "taxes_specify", "Other 16"),
    ("support_others", "support_others_specify", "Other 19"), ("other_expenses", "other_expenses_specify", "Other 21")])
def test_a_specify_text_prints_only_with_its_amount(session, amount_field, text_field, box):
    # text "X" with amount 0 -> box absent; amount 10.00 -> box == "X"

def test_the_new_schedule_j_lines_reach_22a_and_23c(session):
    # wages 3000.00 (answer_household) and personal_care 40.00, taxes_not_deducted 150.00 only
    assert (printed["22a"], printed["22c"], printed["23b"], printed["23c"]) == (
        "190.00", "190.00", "190.00", "2810.00")
```
